# Role Configuration

Roles are project responsibilities. Agent bindings are configurable defaults.

## Default Bindings

| Role | Default agent | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Architect | Codex; scoped Claude exception for the v6.9 site mock-up work on `arch/canonical-v69-pivot` (2026-10-06; see below) | Creates feature maps, causal sequencing, protected seams, and feature-to-task guidance. |
| Planner | Codex (Claude exception ended at the 2026-10-03 21:23 reset; see below) | Converts reviewed feature guidance into small UI-verifiable task definitions. |
| Implementer | Claude | Builds the active task, runs checks, and prepares the review packet. |
| Reviewer | Configurable independent agent; Codex by default (see below) | Reviews work independently from the author. |

## Rules

- Keep role responsibilities stable even if the bound agent changes.
- Do not assume Planner, Architect, or Implementer means a specific vendor unless
  this file says so.
- Implementer may refine implementation steps, but should not redefine feature
  sequencing without Architect or user review.
- Reviewer must be independent from the agent that authored the implementation.
- Claude Code commands in `.claude/commands/` should read this file before
  acting so they can either run the role or prepare a handoff for the configured
  agent.

## Decision Requests Go To The Active Architect

Set by the owner on 2026-10-04 (verbatim in `.agent/owner-T028-closeout-2026-10-04.md`):
"for all decsion requests, let the active architect make them and inform me if
the architect wants me to weigh in as well with its justification and decision".

So the coordinator does not put decision requests to the owner. It sends them
to the Architect bound above, which decides, records the decision with its
justification, and informs the owner. Where the Architect wants the owner to
weigh in, it says so, giving its decision and justification, and the owner may
confirm or overrule. Owner acceptance of a slice (USER_REVIEW_REQUIRED) is not
a decision request and still goes to the owner.

## Architect Was Temporarily Claude, And Is Codex Again

On 2026-09-24 the user set Architect to Claude because Codex had exhausted its
usage limit twice in one day while the task queue waited on an Architect
review. That entry said to revert when capacity allowed. Codex capacity
returned at 18:21 the same day and the binding was reverted at 18:53, so the
exception is closed and the rule below applies again without qualification.

Two Architect passes were produced under the exception, both on
`arch/queue-review-feedback` and both merged: the queue resequencing at
`2970d74` and the hardening-gate removal at `e02d267`. A later reader checking
who authored them should know they were Claude by an explicit, recorded
decision rather than by the coordinator routing around this file.

The lesson worth keeping is the one the exception was written to respect: when
a binding has to change, change it here first and then act. Routing around a
control is worse than the mistake the control prevents.

## Invoking A Codex-Bound Role

A role bound to Codex is run by invoking Codex, not by dispatching the
same-named Claude subagent and merging what it returns. Codex is on PATH and
runs non-interactively:

```
codex exec "<the role brief>"
```

**The obligation is the coordinator's, not the subagent's.** On 2026-09-23 the
Architect correctly flagged three times that it was producing Architect output
while this file bound the role to Codex. Each time the coordinator relayed the
flag to the user as a question and merged the output anyway. A subagent noticing
a binding violation is not a control, because it has already done the work by
the time it can tell anyone. The control is that the coordinator invokes the
bound tool in the first place.

So: before dispatching `assetops-architect` or `assetops-planner`, check the
binding above. If it is Codex, run `codex exec`. The Claude subagents of those
names refuse Codex-bound work rather than producing it.

## The Reviewer Binding, And Its Backup

The Reviewer is **Codex by default**. When Codex is unavailable - usage limit,
outage, or a reset hours away - the backup is a **fresh Claude
`assetops-reviewer` subagent**, and the substitution needs no permission. A
slice waiting a day for quota is worse than a slice reviewed by the weaker of
two independent readers.

**Independence is the rule the backup must not break.** The backup is a NEW
agent instance. It is never the agent that implemented the slice, and never
that agent resumed with a different instruction. An implementer asked to
review its own work will confirm it, which is why the binding exists.

State in the report which reviewer produced it. A Claude review of Claude
implementation is genuinely weaker than a Codex one on the same slice, and a
later reader deciding how much weight to give a verdict needs to know which
they are reading. Where the substitution happened because of quota, say so and
say what was left unjudged, so the question can go back to Codex if it matters
enough to wait for.

## Which Reviewer For Which Round

Set by the user on 2026-09-26 to conserve Codex quota, which has been the
binding constraint on this milestone three times. This is not the same as the
backup rule above: it applies whether or not Codex is available.

**Codex reviews discovery.** The first review of any slice, a contract version
move, a new mechanism or a restructure, and any round where the Implementer had
to choose between legitimate treatments. T020B's R1 was that round - state a
rule for unaligned windows or refuse them at setup - and it needed the stronger
reader.

**A fresh Claude `assetops-reviewer` reviews verification.** A correction round
where an independent reviewer has already named the defects and the fix needs
no design choice: prose, docstrings, claim corrections, test assertions,
deletions. The job is confirming the named items closed and nothing else moved.
Fresh rather than resumed, because there is no context worth preserving for
checking named sentences and a clean reader is worth more.

The honest risk, recorded so it is not discovered later: this relies on the
previous review having found everything discoverable in that area. Four
consecutive rounds on T020B each found something the last missed, which is the
argument against cheap re-reviews. **So a Claude verification pass that finds
something unexpected is a signal to escalate that round back to Codex, not a
cheap verdict to accept.** Finding an unnamed defect means the round was
discovery after all and was misclassified.

Two classification traps worth naming, both seen on T020B:

- **A correction round can widen under the Implementer's hand.** The R1a/R1b
  round was three sentences and a deletion when dispatched; it came back having
  also corrected an unnamed fourth sentence and added a property-based guard
  over thirteen windows. Classify on what came back, not on what was sent.
- **"Small" will drift to "everything" if it is not tested against the two
  lists above.** The question is not how many lines changed. It is whether a
  reader had to decide anything.

## Owner Review Routing From S3 Onward (2026-10-11)

Owner (verbatim, `.agent/owner-review-routing-and-merge-2026-10-11.md`): "codex usage optimizaion is important, so keep the same arragment for S2 going forward". For every slice from S3 onward: a fresh Claude `assetops-reviewer` does the slice's first review and every re-review; small written-list fix rounds run on a Sonnet implementer; one batched Codex review covers each milestone's slices together at the milestone's end; Codex Architect/Planner questions are batched rather than sent per question file. This overrides "Codex reviews discovery" below for per-slice first reviews; Codex remains the Architect, the Planner and the milestone reviewer.

## Do Not Write The Review In The Brief

Recorded on 2026-09-26 at the user's instruction, because this project had
been doing the opposite. Reviewer briefs had grown to eight numbered questions,
each naming the thing to judge and supplying the arguments on both sides. That
is not a brief; it is a draft verdict with blanks. A reviewer steered to eight
questions inspects eight things, and the ninth - the one nobody thought to ask
about - is the one that reaches production.

A brief gives the Reviewer:

- the target: branch, commit, spec, packet, and where prior reviews live;
- the standing criteria it is reviewing against, which live in the task file
  and this project's records, not in the brief;
- **what has already been run and verified**, with results, so quota is not
  spent re-running a green suite. This is factual and does not steer;
- the constraints: do not merge, do not fix, where to write, what not to touch.

A brief does **not** give the Reviewer:

- an enumerated list of what to judge;
- the arguments for or against a conclusion;
- which findings the coordinator considers important;
- a named preferred outcome, or a hint of one.

Pointing at an artifact is not steering; summarising what that artifact says is.
Where a genuine question must be asked - a decision only the user can take, a
constraint the Reviewer cannot infer - ask it plainly and separately, and do not
dress an opinion as a question.

The Implementer's packet already states its own uncertainties and residual
risks. That is the right channel for "look at this": the author declaring what
it is unsure of, in a document the Reviewer reads anyway.

## Sort Findings Before Forwarding Them

Recorded on 2026-09-26 after the user asked whether the Reviewer was applying
`D-2026-09-22-milestone-speed-over-purity`. It was; all three T020A1 reviews
cited it, and Codex used it to carry an HTTP-reachable 500, five overflow
sites and several residuals rather than block on them. **The coordinator was
the leak.**

That decision's stopping rule is narrow. Stop only for a defect expensive to
reverse, a claim that would mislead an Implementer, or a decision only the user
can take. **Everything else is recorded and carried.** A Reviewer that rates a
finding low and does not demand it has already applied the rule; forwarding it
as fix work overrides the Reviewer in the direction the decision forbids.

So when a review returns, the coordinator sorts every finding into block or
carry against those three conditions before putting any of it to the user, and
says which is which. A finding that is carried goes to
`.ai/MILESTONE_REVIEW_BACKLOG.md` with the reason, not into the next round.

T020A1 is the case to remember: five implementation rounds and three review
passes, of which two rounds closed comment counts, a docstring and a Draft
count that had drifted. Each was real. None was a reason to reopen a slice.

## Using Codex Efficiently

Codex quota is **account-level and shared across every model**. On 2026-09-24
it was exhausted twice in one day, the second time mid-review, and switching
to `gpt-6-sol`, `gpt-6-luna`, `gpt-5.6-sol` and `gpt-5.5` all returned the
identical limit with the identical reset time. **You cannot route around an
exhausted limit by changing model.** The only lever is spending less per call,
before the limit is reached.

What it cost that day, measured from the run logs:

| Pass | Tokens | Shell calls | Started |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Reviewer pass 1 | 145,247 | 31 | cold |
| Reviewer pass 2 | 145,568 | 38 | cold |
| Reviewer pass 3 | 25,259, then died | 5 | cold |

Pass 2 spent 145k tokens re-deriving what pass 1 had already established.

### Resume instead of restarting

`codex exec resume <session-id> "<follow-up prompt>"` continues an existing
session with its context intact. `--last` resumes the most recent. The session
id is printed in the header of every run, so **capture it whenever a Codex
role may be returned to** - which is every review, because a review that finds
defects is returned to by definition.

A second pass on the same slice is a follow-up, not a new engagement. Resume
it and say what changed. Start cold only when the subject is genuinely
different or the earlier session would mislead.

**`resume` does not take the same flags as `exec`, and this cost two failed
invocations on 2026-09-25 before it was pinned down.** Two rules:

- Options go **before** the session id: `codex exec resume [OPTIONS]
  <session-id> "<prompt>"`. After it they are parsed as the prompt.
- **`--sandbox` does not exist on `resume`.** Pass it as config instead:
  `-c sandbox_mode="workspace-write"`. `resume` accepts `-c`, `--last`,
  `--all`, `-m`, `--skip-git-repo-check`, `--json`, `-o` and a few others;
  run `codex exec resume --help` rather than assuming a flag carries over.

The working form, verified:

```
codex exec resume -c sandbox_mode="workspace-write" --skip-git-repo-check   <session-id> "<follow-up prompt>" < /dev/null
```

Both failures were argument parsing, rejected before any model call, so they
cost seconds and no quota. The point of recording them is that guidance which
fails on first use is worse than none.

A session resumes across a quota interruption: the third-pass review that died
mid-run at its usage limit was resumed the next day into the same session id
with its context intact.

### Match the model to the work

`-m <model>` selects one. `gpt-6-astra` is the default and the most expensive;
`gpt-6-sol` is the workhorse coding model; `gpt-6-luna` is the cheap one. Full
list: `gpt-6-astra`, `gpt-6-sol`, `gpt-6-luna`, `gpt-5.6-sol`, `gpt-5.6-terra`,
`gpt-5.6-luna`, `gpt-5.5`.

Reserve Astra for genuine architecture and review judgement. Mechanical passes
- a fix round against a written list, a routine Planner expansion, a
consistency sweep - do not need it.

### Never spend a full session on a probe

`~/.codex/config.toml` sets `model_reasoning_effort = "high"` globally, while
the models' own default is `low`. A one-line availability probe therefore runs
at high reasoning. If you must probe, use
`-m gpt-6-luna -c model_reasoning_effort=low`. Better: attempt the real work
and detect the limit from the error, which costs nothing when the limit is
already hit.

### Put verification in the brief, not in the session

Every fact the brief states is work the session does not repeat. Say what you
already ran and what you already verified - test counts, check results,
confirmed line numbers - and say plainly that repeating it is not wanted.
Ask Codex for judgement, not for re-running a green suite.

### Invocation mechanics that have already gone wrong

- **Pass the prompt as an argument, never on stdin.** `codex exec ... - < file`
  blocked for three hours with no output on 2026-09-23. Always redirect
  `< /dev/null`.
- **Do not double-background.** `nohup codex ... &` inside an already
  backgrounded call returns instantly, and the harness reports a completion
  that has not happened. Background the call once.
- **A branch carries a snapshot of these rules.** A session started on a task
  branch reads that branch's `.ai/`, not main's. Merge main into the branch
  before a review if a rule has changed.

## Using The Claude Implementer Efficiently

Set by the owner on 2026-09-29 after T023, T022B and T024 each cost 80-200M
re-read tokens and one to three hours. Measured from the transcripts: cost is
context size times turns, and context grew from resumed agents and from source
code read whole into context. The agent's own rules are in
`.claude/agents/assetops-implementer.md` "Context budget"; these are the
coordinator's:

- **A fresh implementer per build and per fix round. Never resume one.**
  T024's build started at 209k tokens because the read-only planning agent was
  resumed; T023's fix rounds started at 458k. If a slice needs a planning pass,
  the planner writes its plan to `.agent/T0NN-implementer-plan.md` and a fresh
  agent builds from that file.
- **Fix rounds run on Sonnet** (`model: sonnet` on dispatch), briefed with the
  review file and the findings to fix. New slices stay on the session model,
  where design judgement matters.
- **Keep slices small.** A task the implementer sizes at several times a
  previous slice is a split candidate before it is built, not after.
- **Build large slices in phases, a fresh agent per phase.** Set by the owner
  on 2026-09-29 after T025a: bounded reads cut read volume by about two thirds,
  but 508 turns still took context to 467k and cost 142M re-read tokens,
  because cost grows with the square of the turn count. A slice beyond one
  focused change is dispatched as phases (for example contracts and core, then
  model and setup, then Lab and frontend). Each phase ends with committed work
  and a short handoff at `.agent/T0NN-phase-N-handoff.md` (what landed, file
  anchors, what is next, open risks); the coordinator dispatches a fresh agent
  for the next phase from that file. Full suites and the packet belong to the
  last phase.
- **Hand off at about 120k context.** Set by the owner on 2026-10-02 after
  usage showed 66% of tokens spent above 150k context and 67% in implementer
  subagents. An implementer that nears 120k commits what is safe, writes its
  handoff and stops, even mid-phase; a fresh agent continues from the handoff.
  The coordinator plans phases so that none is expected to reach that size,
  splitting a phase into sub-phases (2a, 2b) rather than letting one run long.
- **Backend tests: focused per phase, full once per slice** (OPS-01,
  `backend/TESTING.md`). Each phase runs the focused and adjacent backend files
  it touched; the real-Postgres full suite runs once per slice, on the final
  revision, with the guide's normal command and JUnit zero-skip check.
  Reviewers (Claude or Codex) cite that same-revision full evidence and run
  focused files; they rerun the full suite only for a finding that needs it.
  Before any full run start `tools/keep-awake-during-tests.ps1` and cap the run
  at twice its expected wall time; a run past the cap is stopped and reported,
  never waited on. One full suite on this machine at a time.
- **Mechanical sub-steps run on Sonnet.** Work fixed by an agreed contract or
  handoff - tests written against a settled interface, an AC-by-AC evidence
  pass, closeout records - is dispatched with `model: sonnet`, like fix rounds.
  Steps that decide a design stay on the session model.

## Token Efficiency Without Lowering Quality (owner, 2026-10-18)

Owner: be very efficient without compromising quality, in every session. Lessons
from W1 rework and W2-00..W2-03 (about 30-40% avoidable rework, then 15-20%):

- **All acceptance rules in the brief from the start.** Layout contract,
  recurring-defect checklist, height rule, plain-language rules, proof list.
  Never introduce a rule mid-build; it forces trimming passes on every slice.
- **Build specs answer the known question classes before coding.** A W2+ build
  spec is ready only when it fixes, against the actual code: identifier
  grammars and formats; exact fixture Site/Foundation values and how bindings
  are published; every seam with path:symbol; guard allowlists the new code
  must satisfy (rule 8 writers, sqlalchemy import allowlist, dependency
  direction); refusal codes; freshness/time references; UI empty/unavailable
  copy and the panel map; how Lab/run projections and stored artifacts look.
  W2-02 needed six question rounds because its first spec skipped these.
  Then the Architect does an **implementer dry-read**: walk the spec commit by
  commit against the real code, write down every question a mid-level
  implementer would have to ask (multiplicity such as per-connector channels,
  which component owns each device, new Foundation keys, readiness codes for
  each missing input, cadence sources, fixture Sites vs revisions, migration
  numbering against in-flight slices), and answer each in the spec. W2-05a
  stopped with eight such questions after the gate alone.
- **Size builder runs to one spec commit each**, with a handoff after each, so
  no run dies mid-commit and the next run reads only the handoff.
- **Model by risk.** First review of a new build and design-deciding work on
  the session model; fix rounds, re-reviews (scoped to prior findings) and
  mechanical steps on Sonnet. A capture- or copy-only fix may be verified by
  the coordinator directly (view the evidence, record a short verify note).
- **Serialize slices that edit the same shared UI files**; parallelize only
  disjoint work (backend vs frontend, different domains).
- **One full suite per round, only when no builder is running.** Do not rerun
  full suites after doc-, test- or CSS-only changes; rerun the touched files.
- **Batch Architect/Planner questions into one Codex call**, and fold a
  preemptive sweep of the slice's remaining commits into that same call.
- **No duplicate agents.** Check for a live agent on a worktree before
  dispatching; never reuse a worktree a reviewer is still using.
- **Mockup drives the design; implementers never decide (owner, 2026-10-18).**
  Every spec maps each mock panel, row, column and chip of each owned screen to
  its data source or an honest gap; no mock element is trimmed or redesigned to
  fit the backend. Any implementer choice the spec did not fix is a defect:
  reviewers flag it Medium (High if user-visible vs the mock), and the Architect
  rules it and amends the spec before merge
  (`.agent/owner-fidelity-and-design-ownership-2026-10-18.md`).
- **Briefs point to files; they do not restate them.** Coordinator replies to
  interim agent notifications stay to one line.

## Updating Bindings

Change bindings here when the team wants a different tool to own a role. Record
material changes in `.ai/DECISIONS.md` if they affect workflow, review
independence, or task ownership.

## Open questions (exact; Phase 1 cannot proceed without Q1-Q7)

Q1 (blocker) Per-connector channels. Seven roles are PER EV_CONNECTOR with one channel each, and EV-LAB-001 has c1, c2. `validate_bindings` refuses a channel bound twice (`BINDING_CHANNEL_OVERLAP`, binding_service.py:301) and S2 refuses `EV_CONNECTOR_MISMATCH` unless envelope `connector_id` equals the one bound asset (ledger.py:445). The closed table (design Â§3) names one tuple per signal: `(charger-controller,session-event)`, `(connector-controller,fault-event|state|requested-power|allocated-limit)`, `(connector-meter,energy|power)`, `(site-meter,import)`. What are the exact device_id/signal_id tuples for c1 and for c2? If one channel serves both connectors instead, that changes the overlap refusal and the ledger's one-binding lookup: is that intended? And which Foundation component does each device attach to (a device has one `component_id`): charger-1, the connector, or site-meter-1?

Q2 Readiness state. Does `SITE_IMPORT_NOT_BOUND` make state `CONFIGURATION_INCOMPLETE`, or is it a non-blocking message under `READY_TO_REPORT` like `SOURCE_NOT_CONTACTED`/`COMMISSIONING_UNCONFIRMED`? The spec lists it among the six configuration codes, but calls site import optional for Draft. Confirm that a missing optional fault/power/requested/allocated binding produces none of the eight codes.

Q3 Missing EV properties other than the site import limit (`ev-charger-max-power`, `ev-connector-max-power`, `ev-connector-enabled`, meter resolution/error bound, status/meter cadence, clock bound). Is the publish refused (with which refusal code), or reported as readiness (under which of the eight codes)? "A real Site must author these values" could mean either.

Q4 Cadence. `Role` has no cadence. Cold uses the fixed constant `COLD_PERIODIC_CADENCE_SECONDS`. For EV, is `bindings[].cadence_seconds` the fixed role contract (state 60, energy/power/import/requested/allocated 300, events null), or the Foundation `ev-status-cadence`/`ev-meter-cadence` converted to seconds? What exactly is refused for "cadence > configured ceiling": a property above the role ceiling (60 s / 300 s), or something else? Which property governs the power, site-import, requested and allocated cadences? Is `ev-clock-error-bound` checked against anything in 05a, or only shown?

Q5 Schedule placement and validity. Is `connector_enable_schedules` a new top-level Foundation content key in `FOUNDATION_CONTENT_KEYS`? `site_controls` are ordered member lists and cannot hold it. Is its validity exactly the Foundation revision's effective interval? For a current, open-ended revision, how is "offset matches the zone for every occurrence in the effective date range" checked? Is it checked through a fixed horizon, or refused for any zone with DST? Is the fixture schedule weekly-recurring TUESDAY, or limited to the date 2026-10-06? What is EV-LAB-001's `timezone`? It must give offset 0 (`UTC`?). Must every connector have a schedule entry, and is an ENABLED connector with no entry allowed?

Q6 Fixtures. Are the missing-meter/event/status/limit examples separate Sites (like COLD-001/COLD-002), or later Foundation/binding revisions of EV-LAB-001? Give their site codes if they are Sites. Is `backend/tests/fixtures/w2/ev-lab-001.yaml` created in 05a? The 05b spec says 05b adds it, but the 05a UI needs it.

Q7 Migration. W2-04's `0023` is not on main (head is `0022_w2_cold_assessment`). Should I build Phase 1 now without `0024` and its upgrade/downgrade tests, adding them after main receives `0023`? Or should the slice wait?

Q8 (minor) Energy role kinds. Does `ev_connector_energy` carry `value_kind="INTERVAL_DELTA"` with conversion `IDENTITY_KWH`? Does `IDENTITY_KWH` take no parameters and require source unit kWh? Are the INTERVAL_MEAN roles `NUMBER`/`IDENTITY`? Does `(connector-meter,energy)` have `requires_session=True`? EV bindings snapshot under `V4_MAPPING_PROFILE_ID="simulator-lab-gateway-cold"` in 05a: should they, or under `simulator-lab-gateway-ev/1`?

## Role set, binding and selected read

Current `binding_roles.py:Role.value_kind` and its conversion validator accept NUMBER/STATE/EVENT, while `CONVERSION_KINDS` is IDENTITY/ENUM. The mapping's `requires_session`/connector rules remain the S2 authority.

Extend `sites/binding_roles.py:Role,ROLE_SET_BY_SITE_TYPE,roles_for` with immutable `ev-roles-1`. `ev_session_event` and optional `ev_connector_fault` are EVENT/`event`/IDENTITY per EV_CONNECTOR, one channel each; event kinds and outcomes are the W2-00 v4 allowlist. `ev_connector_state` is STATE/`state`/ENUM per connector, max 60-second cadence with `AVAILABLE|PLUGGED|AUTHORIZING|CHARGING|FAULTED|DISABLED`. `ev_connector_energy` is `INTERVAL_DELTA`/kWh/IDENTITY_KWH per connector, aligned 300-second half-open slots; `ev_connector_power` is optional `INTERVAL_MEAN`/kW. `ev_site_import` is `INTERVAL_MEAN`/kW ONE per feed and is required only for site-power comparison, not outcome/energy. `ev_requested_power` and `ev_allocated_limit` are optional independent `INTERVAL_MEAN`/kW controller channels. Outcome requires session events; fault association requires fault event; supported energy requires energy meter; service-state context requires status. Use `binding_service.py:validate_bindings,publish,acquisition_snapshot` and `binding_alignment.py` to bind exact registered source/device/signal, asset, unit, conversion, clock/cadence and effective interval. Add only the EV tuples to the closed `contracts/assetops_contracts/source_envelope_v4.py:V4_CHANNEL_MAPPINGS` table; `binding_service.py` supplies the five snapshot metadata keys at a new published revision, following W2-02, not on a historical conversion. Do not let one physical signal silently satisfy incompatible roles. Keep v3 bytes and cold binding snapshots unchanged.

`sites/ev_foundation.py` returns `readiness.state=READY_TO_REPORT|CONFIGURATION_INCOMPLETE` and only the design's eight codes: `CONNECTOR_NOT_DECLARED`, `FEED_OR_LIMIT_MISSING`, `METER_NOT_BOUND`, `SESSION_EVENTS_NOT_BOUND`, `STATUS_NOT_BOUND`, `SITE_IMPORT_NOT_BOUND`, `SOURCE_NOT_CONTACTED`, `COMMISSIONING_UNCONFIRMED`. The first six indicate configuration/capability; the last two are separate contact and commissioning notices. The registered Lab Draft gate in `binding_service.py:acquisition_snapshot` must use the selected EV rule rather than mini-grid electrical gaps: block absent connector/feed/limit or required session/status/energy bindings; optional site import/fault/power roles do not block a run that does not claim their capability. A wrong role set or failed effective binding refuses Draft. No test-only gap lift.

Add selected `GET /api/sites/{site_code}/ev/foundation?at=<UTC>` beside the existing cold Foundation router/composition (`rg 'build_cold_foundation_router' backend/assetops_backend`). JSON decimal quantities are decimal strings; unknowns are null, not zero. Use the cold selected-read `Access`/source gate: 401 `UNAUTHENTICATED`, 403 `SITE_SCOPE_FORBIDDEN`, 404 `SITE_NOT_FOUND` including wrong type after access check, 422 `EV_SELECTION_INVALID`, 503 `EV_FOUNDATION_UNAVAILABLE`; malformed/extra query fields refuse. `contact` maps S7 CURRENTâ†’RECENT, STALEâ†’STALE, absent diagnosticâ†’NEVER with reason and null `as_of`; selected gateway identity comes from registration, not mock GW-EV-01. ## Causal pack and public transform

Register `ev-charger/1` in the closed dispatch used by `host/execution_adapter.py`, `host/lab_execution.py` and simulator kernel, beside `cold-thermal/1`; never use fallback to fuel/mini-grid on unknown identity. Add pure pack under `simulator/assetops_simulator/packs/ev_charger.py` using the neutral `packs/errors.py:PhysicallyUnacceptable`. Start `UNPLUGGED`, no session/fault, 0 kWh. Per UTC minute apply forcing sorted `(time,connector,kind)`, with PLUG before FAULT at equal time. Plug assigns the deterministic scoped session ID; authorization lasts one minute; accepted charging emits START. Denial emits no START. Active fault emits FAULT+ABORT; pre-START fault emits FAULT only. Explicit unplug after active charge and without fault emits END. Grid loss pauses power; it never fabricates ABORT. Clear requires forcing. Available shared kW is `max(0,80-base_load)`, allocated in 1 kW round-robin quanta by connector ID with persistent pointer, bounded by request, connector and charger ratings; exact decimal `energy_next=energy+allocated_kw/60`. Guard every state transition, monotone time, finite nonnegative energy, allocation sum/limits, residual, and one START/terminal per ID. Private vehicle request/forcing/oracle remains in world data only.

`ev-publication/1` in the simulator observation/publish seam produces state on transition and each minute; connector `INTERVAL_DELTA` kWh and optional power/site import means on aligned five-minute half-open slots; session/fault events at transitions. Split boundary slots into nonoverlapping subintervals. At each boundary round cumulative kWh half-even to 0.001, subtract rounded boundaries, preserve additive total. `source_time=interval_end` for intervals. Every connector record carries nonnull `session_id,connector_id`; Site meter uses explicit null session/connector. `connector_id` equals the bound asset ID; no charger serial or customer ID becomes a session ID. W2-00 `EVENT_ID_CONFLICT`, `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT`, `EV_CONNECTOR_MISMATCH`, `EV_DELTA_*` rules are unchanged. IDs are `ev-` plus lowercase SHA-256 of canonical JSON array `[scenario_sha256,device_id,signal_id,transition_ordinal]`; correlations use `ev-` plus SHA-256 of `[scenario_sha256,"session",session_id]`. They satisfy v4 `[A-Za-z0-9._-]`; only `report_key` may use `ev:` prefix, following cold's separate report-key grammar. Retransmission preserves bytes. `ev-observation/1` drops/holds/replaces public reports only; late END keeps source time/ID and publishes at 09:05. The contradictory case adds a distinct public GOOD END at 08:30 while the model emits only its genuine END at 08:31. No direct accepted-row/Finding seed.

Reuse `contracts/assetops_contracts/gateway_report.py:DeviceReportV2`; gateway `GatewayService._admit_in` remains sole v4 envelope/sequence/message allocator. Add the EV tuple matrix `(charger-controller,session-event)`, `(connector-controller,fault-event|state|requested-power|allocated-limit)`, `(connector-meter,energy|power)`, `(site-meter,import)` with exact family/kind/unit/role from design Â§3. Registered Stage sends v2 reports to `RegisteredGatewayPublisher.publish_in_boundary_order`; `GatewayService.seal,release_run,record_receipt` and SQLite outbox then authenticated Batch V2/S2 ledger carry them. Keep v1/v3 and cold canonical bytes unchanged.

## Open questions (exact; Phase 1 cannot proceed without Q1-Q7)

Q1 (blocker) Per-connector channels. Seven roles are PER EV_CONNECTOR with one channel each, and EV-LAB-001 has c1, c2. `validate_bindings` refuses a channel bound twice (`BINDING_CHANNEL_OVERLAP`, binding_service.py:301) and S2 refuses `EV_CONNECTOR_MISMATCH` unless envelope `connector_id` equals the one bound asset (ledger.py:445). The closed table (design Â§3) names one tuple per signal: `(charger-controller,session-event)`, `(connector-controller,fault-event|state|requested-power|allocated-limit)`, `(connector-meter,energy|power)`, `(site-meter,import)`. What are the exact device_id/signal_id tuples for c1 and for c2? If one channel serves both connectors instead, that changes the overlap refusal and the ledger's one-binding lookup: is that intended? And which Foundation component does each device attach to (a device has one `component_id`): charger-1, the connector, or site-meter-1?

Q2 Readiness state. Does `SITE_IMPORT_NOT_BOUND` make state `CONFIGURATION_INCOMPLETE`, or is it a non-blocking message under `READY_TO_REPORT` like `SOURCE_NOT_CONTACTED`/`COMMISSIONING_UNCONFIRMED`? The spec lists it among the six configuration codes, but calls site import optional for Draft. Confirm that a missing optional fault/power/requested/allocated binding produces none of the eight codes.

Q3 Missing EV properties other than the site import limit (`ev-charger-max-power`, `ev-connector-max-power`, `ev-connector-enabled`, meter resolution/error bound, status/meter cadence, clock bound). Is the publish refused (with which refusal code), or reported as readiness (under which of the eight codes)? "A real Site must author these values" could mean either.

Q4 Cadence. `Role` has no cadence. Cold uses the fixed constant `COLD_PERIODIC_CADENCE_SECONDS`. For EV, is `bindings[].cadence_seconds` the fixed role contract (state 60, energy/power/import/requested/allocated 300, events null), or the Foundation `ev-status-cadence`/`ev-meter-cadence` converted to seconds? What exactly is refused for "cadence > configured ceiling": a property above the role ceiling (60 s / 300 s), or something else? Which property governs the power, site-import, requested and allocated cadences? Is `ev-clock-error-bound` checked against anything in 05a, or only shown?

Q5 Schedule placement and validity. Is `connector_enable_schedules` a new top-level Foundation content key in `FOUNDATION_CONTENT_KEYS`? `site_controls` are ordered member lists and cannot hold it. Is its validity exactly the Foundation revision's effective interval? For a current, open-ended revision, how is "offset matches the zone for every occurrence in the effective date range" checked? Is it checked through a fixed horizon, or refused for any zone with DST? Is the fixture schedule weekly-recurring TUESDAY, or limited to the date 2026-10-06? What is EV-LAB-001's `timezone`? It must give offset 0 (`UTC`?). Must every connector have a schedule entry, and is an ENABLED connector with no entry allowed?

Q6 Fixtures. Are the missing-meter/event/status/limit examples separate Sites (like COLD-001/COLD-002), or later Foundation/binding revisions of EV-LAB-001? Give their site codes if they are Sites. Is `backend/tests/fixtures/w2/ev-lab-001.yaml` created in 05a? The 05b spec says 05b adds it, but the 05a UI needs it.

Q7 Migration. W2-04's `0023` is not on main (head is `0022_w2_cold_assessment`). Should I build Phase 1 now without `0024` and its upgrade/downgrade tests, adding them after main receives `0023`? Or should the slice wait?

Q8 (minor) Energy role kinds. Does `ev_connector_energy` carry `value_kind="INTERVAL_DELTA"` with conversion `IDENTITY_KWH`? Does `IDENTITY_KWH` take no parameters and require source unit kWh? Are the INTERVAL_MEAN roles `NUMBER`/`IDENTITY`? Does `(connector-meter,energy)` have `requires_session=True`? EV bindings snapshot under `V4_MAPPING_PROFILE_ID="simulator-lab-gateway-cold"` in 05a: should they, or under `simulator-lab-gateway-ev/1`?

Define properties and carriers exactly: `ev-site-import-limit` kW on `AC_BUS`; `ev-charger-max-power` kW on `EV_CHARGER`; `ev-connector-max-power` kW and `ev-connector-enabled` state on `EV_CONNECTOR`; `ev-meter-resolution` and `ev-meter-error-bound` kWh on `EV_CONNECTOR`; `ev-status-cadence` and `ev-meter-cadence` min plus `ev-clock-error-bound` s on `EV_CONNECTOR`. The first three limits, meter resolution/cadences and clock bound are strictly positive finite values; meter error bound nonnegative finite. The selected fixture has feed limit 80 kW, two connector/charger ratings 60 kW, resolution/error bound 0.001 kWh, status cadence 1 min, meter cadence 5 min and clock error bound 30 s. A real Site must author these values; the pack has no silent production default. Represent the connector enable schedule as a typed, time-valid Foundation control named `connector_enable_schedules`, an array of `{connector_asset_id,time_zone,weekly:[{weekday,local_start,local_end,utc_offset_minutes}]}`. `weekday` is uppercase MONDAYâ€¦SUNDAY, local times are `HH:MM`, intervals are half-open within one local day, `time_zone` is the Site IANA zone, and the explicit offset must match that zone for every occurrence in the Foundation revision's effective date range; publish a new revision when the offset changes. The fixture declares both connectors enabled TUESDAY 08:00â€“16:00, UTC offset 0, for 2026-10-06. Older revisions remain selectable and never inherit new limits/schedule. A configured service window is not observed availability.

## Role set, binding and selected read

Current `binding_roles.py:Role.value_kind` and its conversion validator accept NUMBER/STATE/EVENT, while `CONVERSION_KINDS` is IDENTITY/ENUM. Extend `sites/binding_roles.py:Role,ROLE_SET_BY_SITE_TYPE,roles_for` with immutable `ev-roles-1`. `ev_session_event` and optional `ev_connector_fault` are EVENT/`event`/IDENTITY per EV_CONNECTOR, one channel each; event kinds and outcomes are the W2-00 v4 allowlist. `ev_connector_state` is STATE/`state`/ENUM per connector, max 60-second cadence with `AVAILABLE|PLUGGED|AUTHORIZING|CHARGING|FAULTED|DISABLED`. `ev_connector_energy` is `INTERVAL_DELTA`/kWh/IDENTITY_KWH per connector, aligned 300-second half-open slots; `ev_connector_power` is optional `INTERVAL_MEAN`/kW. `ev_site_import` is `INTERVAL_MEAN`/kW ONE per feed and is required only for site-power comparison, not outcome/energy. `ev_requested_power` and `ev_allocated_limit` are optional independent `INTERVAL_MEAN`/kW controller channels. Outcome requires session events; fault association requires fault event; supported energy requires energy meter; service-state context requires status. Use `binding_service.py:validate_bindings,publish,acquisition_snapshot` and `binding_alignment.py` to bind exact registered source/device/signal, asset, unit, conversion, clock/cadence and effective interval. Add only the EV tuples to the closed `contracts/assetops_contracts/source_envelope_v4.py:V4_CHANNEL_MAPPINGS` table; `binding_service.py` supplies the five snapshot metadata keys at a new published revision, following W2-02, not on a historical conversion. Do not let one physical signal silently satisfy incompatible roles. Keep v3 bytes and cold binding snapshots unchanged.

`sites/ev_foundation.py` returns `readiness.state=READY_TO_REPORT|CONFIGURATION_INCOMPLETE` and only the design's eight codes: `CONNECTOR_NOT_DECLARED`, `FEED_OR_LIMIT_MISSING`, `METER_NOT_BOUND`, `SESSION_EVENTS_NOT_BOUND`, `STATUS_NOT_BOUND`, `SITE_IMPORT_NOT_BOUND`, `SOURCE_NOT_CONTACTED`, `COMMISSIONING_UNCONFIRMED`. The first six indicate configuration/capability; the last two are separate contact and commissioning notices. ## Token Efficiency Without Lowering Quality (owner, 2026-10-18)

Owner: be very efficient without compromising quality, in every session. Lessons
from W1 rework and W2-00..W2-03 (about 30-40% avoidable rework, then 15-20%):

- **All acceptance rules in the brief from the start.** Layout contract,
  recurring-defect checklist, height rule, plain-language rules, proof list.
  Never introduce a rule mid-build; it forces trimming passes on every slice.
- **Build specs answer the known question classes before coding.** A W2+ build
  spec is ready only when it fixes, against the actual code: identifier
  grammars and formats; exact fixture Site/Foundation values and how bindings
  are published; every seam with path:symbol; guard allowlists the new code
  must satisfy (rule 8 writers, sqlalchemy import allowlist, dependency
  direction); refusal codes; freshness/time references; UI empty/unavailable
  copy and the panel map; how Lab/run projections and stored artifacts look.
  W2-02 needed six question rounds because its first spec skipped these.
  Then the Architect does an **implementer dry-read**: walk the spec commit by
  commit against the real code, write down every question a mid-level
  implementer would have to ask (multiplicity such as per-connector channels,
  which component owns each device, new Foundation keys, readiness codes for
  each missing input, cadence sources, fixture Sites vs revisions, migration
  numbering against in-flight slices), and answer each in the spec. W2-05a
  stopped with eight such questions after the gate alone.
- **Size builder runs to one spec commit each**, with a handoff after each, so
  no run dies mid-commit and the next run reads only the handoff.
- **Model by risk.** First review of a new build and design-deciding work on
  the session model; fix rounds, re-reviews (scoped to prior findings) and
  mechanical steps on Sonnet. A capture- or copy-only fix may be verified by
  the coordinator directly (view the evidence, record a short verify note).
- **Serialize slices that edit the same shared UI files**; parallelize only
  disjoint work (backend vs frontend, different domains).
- **One full suite per round, only when no builder is running.** Do not rerun
  full suites after doc-, test- or CSS-only changes; rerun the touched files.
- **Batch Architect/Planner questions into one Codex call**, and fold a

20:W2-00 enforces one accepted START per `(tenant,site,connector asset,session_id)` and returns `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT`/`CONFLICT` with the first START evidence reference for a distinct second START. 24:Register immutable `ev-roles-1` in the site-type role registry; bind by effective Foundation and source mapping revisions (`backend/assetops_backend/sites/binding_roles.py:17-27,117-130`). Topology is Site feed → Site import meter → EV charger → EV connector; each connector has exactly one parent charger, each charger one feed, each asset ID distinct, no topology cycles or cross-Site links. Charger/connector serial labels are display metadata, never session IDs. Site import limit and charger/connector ratings are effective Foundation facts, not measured capacity. Connector enable schedule is a time-valid authored schedule in Foundation (`weekly local intervals`, Site timezone, explicit UTC offset resolution for DST); W2 uses it for configured service hours only, not an availability percentage. Reject overlapping intervals, nonexistent/ambiguous local instants and enabled connector without a declared charger/feed/meter. No stationary battery unless explicitly declared.

42:**Discrete model.** One-minute UTC step, exact decimal arithmetic; default fixture has two 60 kW connectors, 80 kW site import cap, both enabled [08:00,16:00) UTC on 2026-10-06, site non-EV base load 0 kW. State per connector: `UNPLUGGED|PLUGGED|AUTHORIZING|CHARGING|FAULTED`, session ID or null, authorization result, requested kW, allocated kW, meter accumulator kWh, fault latch. Private forcing: plug/unplug, authorization accept/deny, request schedule, fault/clear, grid interruption, publication dropout/latency. Initial unplugged, no session/fault, accumulator 0. At each minute: apply forcing ordered `(time,connector,kind)`; a plug assigns next deterministic scoped session ID; authorization takes 1 minute; acceptance emits START when CHARGING begins; denial produces no START and is a contextual observed state, not a failed session. Allocate shared available power `max(0,site_limit-base_load)` by connector ID ascending in round-robin 1 kW quanta among charging connectors up to `min(request,connector_limit,charger_limit)`; persist round-robin pointer so no connector monopolizes successive minutes. `E_next=E+allocated_kW/60`, exact kWh. A fault latches FAULTED, emits FAULT and ABORT for an active started session; explicit user unplug emits END (completed) only after active charge and no fault; grid interruption pauses allocation and connector state, it does not invent ABORT. Fault clear requires explicit forcing and emits CLEAR; a new plug creates a new session. Simultaneous END/ABORT or START twice for one ID is prohibited by simulator sanity guard. Fail run on negative energy, allocated sum above available/site/connector limits, energy residual, state with no valid session, duplicate conflicting forcing or non-monotone time.
44:**Public transform.** `ev-publication/1`: connector state at transitions and every minute; connector interval energy delta over aligned five-minute `[start,end)` slots (including zero while an active accepted session pauses), with session ID in the W2-00 v4 measurement payload; power mean per slot and site import mean per slot; session/fault events at observed transitions. Every v4 measurement contains `session_id,connector_id`; both are nonnull for connector energy and explicit null on cold/site channels. Connector ID must equal the bound EV_CONNECTOR asset; S2 refuses `EV_CONNECTOR_MISMATCH`. `source_time=interval_end` for interval reports. Split a session boundary inside a slot into nonoverlapping subintervals. Round accumulator to 0.001 kWh half-even at both ends and difference rounded values, preserving additive deltas; meter bound 0.001 kWh per boundary. Event IDs derive from scenario SHA, channel and transition ordinal; retransmission preserves IDs/bytes. Observation gap drops a channel report, never changes the world; delayed report retains source time and message identity. Mapping `simulator-lab-gateway-ev/1` exactly allows `(charger-controller,session-event)`, `(connector-controller,fault-event)`, `(connector-controller,state)`, `(connector-meter,energy)`, `(connector-meter,power)`, `(site-meter,import)`, `(connector-controller,requested-power)`, `(connector-controller,allocated-limit)` with listed kinds/units/cadences (`simulator/assetops_simulator/gateway/mappings.py:22-46,87-107`).

55:All scenarios use `ev-scenario/1`, frozen window/seed/profile and initial unplugged/no-session/no-fault/0 kWh state. Enabled schedule is [08:00,16:00) UTC; c1/c2 each max 60 kW and Site cap 80 kW, base load 0. The single charging connector receives 60 kW every active minute. `simulator/assetops_simulator/observation/transform.py` owns `ev-observation/1` hold/drop/fault variants; they alter public reports only. No mock 12/10/1/1, 120 kWh or $36 is injected (`frontend/mockups/v69/screens.js:433-450`). `Role.value_kind` includes `EVENT` and `INTERVAL_DELTA`; EVENT conversion is `IDENTITY` with outcome allowlist, delta conversion is `IDENTITY_KWH` with exact decimal and no counter interpretation. Lab persisted-state validator uses the EV connector state vocabulary of `ev-charger/1`, not the mini-grid generator lifecycle.
59:Select only accepted, GOOD, clock-attested records with valid effective Foundation/binding and exact `(tenant,site,charger,connector,session_id)`; EVENT session ID comes from W2-00, energy identity from the v4 measurement extension above. Require one START, at most one terminal END or ABORT, ordered by source time with <=30-second declared cross-source clock error; a terminal without START remains orphan context, not a session. An END is COMPLETED only when same scoped ID, no competing ABORT, and no contradictory state; explicit ABORT proves OBSERVED_ABORT, while contemporaneous fault on the same connector/session is additionally required for a **fault-associated** Finding. Fault alone does not prove an abort. Missing terminal is INCOMPLETE regardless of apparently idle status; conflicting good terminal/start times or incompatible connector identity is CONTRADICTORY. `SUSPECT` and unbound reports are context/excluded with reasons, never settle outcomes. Repeat events are deduped by W2-00 and cross-tenant/session collisions cannot join.
61:For each session, intersect GOOD connector `INTERVAL_DELTA` reports with `[start,terminal)` and same session ID; accept only wholly contained, nonoverlapping half-open intervals with finite `0<=kWh<=connector_max_kW*duration_hours+2*meter_error_bound`, and matching exact source. Do **not** pro-rate a report spanning a boundary, choose between conflicting duplicates, substitute power integration or infer zero for an absent slot. `supported_kwh=sum(exact accepted deltas)`; `unknown_meter_seconds` is the union duration of uncovered active 5-minute slots, clipped to session bounds. A delivered-energy total is **complete** only if every active slot is covered (partial boundary slots must have a split report) and no contradiction; otherwise report known partial sum with `energy_complete=false` and unknown time, never call it total delivered. If no supported intervals, quantity null rather than zero. Session outcome counts use START denominator, separately completed/observed abort/incomplete/contradictory; orphan records are separate. No availability percentage or site constraint cause in W2. Synchronized site import below limit is context only; a power-constraint claim requires independent accepted requested and allocated-limit signals plus site import over the same slots, and W2 still labels competing causes unknown (`.agent/architect-W2-design.md:37-49`).
63:`ev-session/1` assessment basis SHA sorts accepted evidence IDs+content digests, excluded/context IDs+reasons, effective Foundation/binding/mapping IDs, model/rule versions, `[from,to)`, `known_at` and source classification. Session revision and its evidence/interval links append atomically with `assessment_revision` and any FindingRevision. A fault-abort Finding uses the stable key above, with exact fault/abort/START evidence and limitation “fault observed; component cause and unmet demand unknown.” A completed late END supersedes prior INCOMPLETE by new revision; a contradictory terminal withdraws previous current conclusion. Worker enqueue on **every** newly accepted relevant EV report, including late source time; until latest applicable job settles, previous projection reads `PENDING_RECOMPUTE`, with historical exact revision separately selectable. For current status, latest needed connector state <=2 minutes old at read time and terminal/event sources within declared cadence; old closed sessions may remain historical conclusions. Current service-window coverage uses one-minute status slots and explicit `known_seconds/unknown_seconds`; no available-time inference over gaps >2 minutes. Source gate follows S4-05 exact revision provenance; generated and authored evidence are labelled and mixed/unknown blocks a current positive (`.ai/DECISIONS.md:5-15,4165-4188`).
67:`ev_gross_tariff_per_kwh` is a Site time-valid, recorded revision in `currency/kWh`, with authored basis/source; no default and no billing/collection identity. A window may price only supported energy in completed or observed-abort sessions, each with complete meter coverage and settled current assessment; no session interval is counted twice. For every included meter interval, select exactly one tariff revision whose **valid** range covers it and whose **recorded** range is visible at `known_at`; W2 v1 requires the same revision and currency across the entire priced window, otherwise `UNPRICED/PRICE_GAP_OR_CHANGE` (no averaging). Formula `amount = Σ exact interval kWh × tariff value`, unrounded in storage; display rounding only. `quantity_kwh` and exact session revision/accepted meter evidence/tariff revision IDs are pinned. No lost-sales estimate: private requested power, incomplete sessions and fault downtime do not measure counterfactual demand. An aborted session can contribute **delivered** pre-abort energy, never lost energy. W1 LR current-money gate and S4-05 coherent source gate apply: pending/late/revised assessment, conflicting meter, historical cutoff, mixed/unknown provenance, unsupported tariff, or missing price → `WITHHELD` or `UNPRICED`, null amount (not zero). Historical amount may appear labelled historical only under its exact old basis, never as today's current money. `overlap_group='ev:'+sha256(canonical(tenant_id,site_id,window_start,window_end,tariff_revision_id))` prevents adding the same meter intervals through multiple Findings; Site scenario is one window revision, not per-Finding additive totals (`backend/migrations/versions/0010_financial_assumptions.py:38-102`; `.ai/DECISIONS.md:5-15`).

def header_message_id(text: object) -> str:
    """The valid header's message ID for an ordinal refusal, or '' when the header itself is invalid."""
    try:
        return read_envelope_header(text).message_id
    except EnvelopeRejected:
        return ""


# --- closed channel table -------------------------------------------------------

V4_MAPPING_PROFILE_ID = "simulator-lab-gateway-cold"
V4_MAPPING_PROFILE_VERSION = 1


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ChannelMapping:
    """One closed `(device_id, signal_id)` entry: the family/measurement it carries and its v4 metadata."""

    family: str
    measurement: str | None
    allowed_record_kinds: tuple[str, ...]
    requires_correlation: bool
    requires_session: bool
    allowed_units: tuple[str, ...]
    #: W2-01 (W2-00 review Low 2, design section 3 W2-02 channel closure): the exact `cold-roles-1` role the
    #: channel serves. None only for the reserved EV tuples, whose roles W2-05a defines.
    role: str | None = None

    @property
    def allowed_outcomes(self) -> dict[str, list[str]]:
        return {k: sorted(V4_EVENT_OUTCOMES[k]) for k in self.allowed_record_kinds}

    def metadata(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """The five snapshot binding-row keys this entry requires, in their canonical spelling."""
        return {
            "allowed_record_kinds": sorted(self.allowed_record_kinds),
            "allowed_outcomes": self.allowed_outcomes,
            "requires_correlation": self.requires_correlation,
            "requires_session": self.requires_session,
            "allowed_units": sorted(self.allowed_units),
        }


def _point(unit: str) -> ChannelMapping:
    return ChannelMapping("MEASUREMENT", "STATE_AT_INSTANT", (), False, False, (unit,))


def _events(*kinds: str, session: bool = False, units: tuple[str, ...] = ()) -> ChannelMapping:
    return ChannelMapping("EVENT", None, tuple(kinds), True, session, units)


#: Design section 4: the closed cold mapping plus the two reserved EV tuples. Same signal ID on a different
#: device is not an alias. `simulator-lab-gateway-cold/1`, schema 4.
V4_CHANNEL_MAPPINGS: Mapping[tuple[str, str], ChannelMapping] = {
    ("chamber-probe", "temperature"): replace(_point("degC"), role="chamber_temperature"),
    ("ambient-probe", "temperature"): replace(_point("degC"), role="ambient_temperature"),
    ("grid-meter", "state"): replace(_point(DISCRETE_UNIT), role="grid_state"),
    ("generator-controller", "state"): replace(_point(DISCRETE_UNIT), role="generator_state"),
    ("compressor-controller", "state"): replace(_point(DISCRETE_UNIT), role="compressor_state"),
    ("backup-bus-meter", "state"): replace(_point(DISCRETE_UNIT), role="backup_bus_state"),
    ("compressor-meter", "power"): ChannelMapping("MEASUREMENT", "INTERVAL_MEAN", (), False, False, ("kW",),
                                                  role="compressor_power"),
    ("door-sensor", "event"): replace(_events("DOOR_OPEN", "DOOR_CLOSE"), role="door_event"),
    ("generator-controller", "start-event"): replace(_events("START_REQUEST", "START_RESULT"),
                                                     role="backup_start_event"),
    ("transfer-controller", "event"): replace(_events("TRANSFER_RESULT"), role="transfer_result"),
    ("grid-meter", "event"): replace(_events("POWER_LOSS", "POWER_RESTORED"), role="grid_power_event"),
    ("operator-log", "temperature-event"): replace(_events("MANUAL_LOG", units=(MANUAL_LOG_UNIT,)),
                                                   role="manual_temperature"),
    ("charger-controller", "session-event"): _events("EV_SESSION_START", "EV_SESSION_END", "EV_SESSION_ABORT",
                                                     session=True),
    ("connector-controller", "fault-event"): _events("EV_CONNECTOR_FAULT", "EV_CONNECTOR_CLEAR"),
}

V4_BINDING_METADATA_KEYS = ("allowed_record_kinds", "allowed_outcomes", "requires_correlation",
                            "requires_session", "allowed_units")


def canonical_binding_metadata(row: Mapping[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
    """A snapshot binding row's five v4 keys in canonical spelling, or None when any is absent or ill-typed."""
    if any(row.get(k) is None for k in V4_BINDING_METADATA_KEYS):
        return None
    kinds, outcomes, units = row["allowed_record_kinds"], row["allowed_outcomes"], row["allowed_units"]
    if (not isinstance(kinds, (list, tuple)) or not all(type(k) is str for k in kinds)
            or not isinstance(units, (list, tuple)) or not all(type(u) is str for u in units)
            or not isinstance(outcomes, Mapping)
            or not all(type(k) is str and isinstance(v, (list, tuple)) and all(type(o) is str for o in v)
                       for k, v in outcomes.items())
            or type(row["requires_correlation"]) is not bool or type(row["requires_session"]) is not bool):
        return None
    return {
        "allowed_record_kinds": sorted(kinds),
        "allowed_outcomes": {k: sorted(v) for k, v in outcomes.items()},
        "requires_correlation": row["requires_correlation"],
        "requires_session": row["requires_session"],
        "allowed_units": sorted(units),
    }

"""The code-owned MINIGRID signal role vocabulary (S1-02).

Decided by `D-2026-10-08-s1-bindings-roles-and-snapshot` (Architect answers
Q1, Q3 and Q4 in `.agent/architect-S1-02-answers-2026-10-08.md`). Every
vocabulary choice lives here; the binding mechanism does not name a role.

A role names what a bound channel means for analysis. The canonical unit is
fixed per role. A role is either a channel role (bound to one registered
device signal) or a configuration role (covered by a Foundation control, not
by a channel).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

#: S1-02's vocabulary. Kept readable for every binding set recorded under it.
ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_1 = "minigrid-roles-1"
#: S3-01 (`D-2026-10-11-s3-dispatch-interval-assessment`, Architect batch 5
#: "S3-01 Planner questions"): roles-1 plus the two optional capability
#: channels a dispatch Candidate needs. Observed `pv_power` and
#: `battery_power` are not these capabilities.
ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_2 = "minigrid-roles-2"
#: S6-02a (`D-2026-10-16-s6-verification`, `D-2026-10-16-s602-questions`):
#: roles-2 plus the requested and delivered critical-load power a reserve
#: trial's service guardrail needs. A Site opts in by publishing with this
#: role set explicitly; new publications still default to roles-2.
ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_3 = "minigrid-roles-3"
#: The set a new MINIGRID binding publication is recorded under.
ROLE_SET_MINIGRID = ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_2
#: W2-01 (`.agent/W2-technical-design-cold.md` section 3, `D-2026-10-17-w2-01-answers`
#: and its W2-02 channel closure): the immutable COLDCHAIN vocabulary.
ROLE_SET_COLD_1 = "cold-roles-1"

#: The canonical generator states an ENUM conversion may map to.
GENERATOR_STATES: tuple[str, ...] = ("STOPPED", "STARTING", "RUNNING", "FAILED")
#: W2-01 role-specific ENUM member sets (`D-2026-10-17-w2-01-answers` B).
COMPRESSOR_STATES: tuple[str, ...] = ("OFF", "STARTING", "RUNNING", "FAILED")
GRID_STATES: tuple[str, ...] = ("AVAILABLE", "LOST")
BUS_STATES: tuple[str, ...] = ("POWERED", "UNPOWERED")

#: The nonquantitative declared unit of an event channel (`SIGNAL_UNITS`).
EVENT_UNIT = "event"

#: One binding per Site, or one per asset of the role's asset type.
ONE = "ONE"
PER_ASSET = "PER_ASSET"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Role:
    role: str
    label: str
    asset_type: str
    canonical_unit: str
    value_kind: str  # NUMBER | STATE | EVENT
    cardinality: str  # ONE | PER_ASSET
    required: bool
    #: "CHANNEL" roles are bound to a device signal; "FOUNDATION_CONTROL"
    #: roles are covered by a configured Foundation control (decision Q3).
    covered_by: str = "CHANNEL"
    #: S6-02a: a ONE role describing the Site's critical load - the first
    #: member of the Foundation's `critical-load-priority` control. The
    #: control identifies the one asset, so several LOAD components are not
    #: an ambiguity; a binding naming any other load is refused.
    critical_load: bool = False
    #: W2-01: a STATE role's own ENUM member set. Empty means the generator
    #: states every MINIGRID state role has always mapped to.
    states: tuple[str, ...] = ()
    #: W2-01: an EVENT role's exact v4 record kinds; the outcomes allowed for
    #: each are the closed v4 kind-to-outcome table's.
    event_kinds: tuple[str, ...] = ()

    @property
    def enum_states(self) -> tuple[str, ...]:
        return self.states or GENERATOR_STATES


MINIGRID_ROLES: tuple[Role, ...] = (
    Role("generator_state", "Generator state", "GENERATOR", "state", "STATE", ONE, True),
    Role("generator_power", "Generator power", "GENERATOR", "kW", "NUMBER", ONE, True),
    Role("feeder_demand", "Feeder demand", "LOAD", "kW", "NUMBER", PER_ASSET, True),
    Role("battery_soc", "Battery state of charge", "BATTERY", "%", "NUMBER", ONE, True),
    Role(
        "battery_reserve", "Battery reserve", "BATTERY", "%", "NUMBER", ONE, True,
        covered_by="FOUNDATION_CONTROL",
    ),
    Role("pv_power", "Solar inverter power", "INVERTER", "kW", "NUMBER", ONE, False),
    Role("pv_irradiance", "Solar irradiance", "PV_ARRAY", "W/m2", "NUMBER", ONE, False),
    Role("battery_power", "Battery converter power", "POWER_CONVERSION_SYSTEM", "kW", "NUMBER", ONE, False),
    Role("fuel_level", "Fuel level", "FUEL_TANK", "L", "NUMBER", ONE, False),
)

MINIGRID_ROLES_2: tuple[Role, ...] = MINIGRID_ROLES + (
    Role("pv_available_power", "Solar available power", "INVERTER", "kW", "NUMBER", ONE, False),
    Role(
        "pcs_discharge_capability", "Battery converter discharge capability",
        "POWER_CONVERSION_SYSTEM", "kW", "NUMBER", ONE, False,
    ),
)

MINIGRID_ROLES_3: tuple[Role, ...] = MINIGRID_ROLES_2 + (
    Role(
        "critical_load_requested_power", "Critical load requested power", "LOAD", "kW", "NUMBER", ONE, False,
        critical_load=True,
    ),
    Role(
        "critical_load_delivered_power", "Critical load delivered power", "LOAD", "kW", "NUMBER", ONE, False,
        critical_load=True,
    ),
)

#: `cold-roles-1`, canonical per the design's role table and the W2-02 channel
#: closure: one `backup_start_event` channel carries both start kinds, and
#: `grid_power_event` is separate from `grid_state`. The two room policy roles
#: are Foundation controls (typed `cold-policy-low/high` properties), never
#: channel bindings. Immutable: a change is a new named version.
COLD_ROLES_1: tuple[Role, ...] = (
    Role("chamber_temperature", "Chamber temperature", "TEMPERATURE_PROBE", "degC", "NUMBER", PER_ASSET, True),
    Role(
        "cold_policy_low", "Temperature policy low", "COLD_ROOM", "degC", "NUMBER", PER_ASSET, True,
        covered_by="FOUNDATION_CONTROL",
    ),
    Role(
        "cold_policy_high", "Temperature policy high", "COLD_ROOM", "degC", "NUMBER", PER_ASSET, True,
        covered_by="FOUNDATION_CONTROL",
    ),
    Role(
        "compressor_state", "Compressor state", "COMPRESSOR", "state", "STATE", PER_ASSET, True,
        states=COMPRESSOR_STATES,
    ),
    Role("compressor_power", "Compressor power", "COMPRESSOR", "kW", "NUMBER", PER_ASSET, False),
    Role("grid_state", "Grid state", "METER", "state", "STATE", ONE, True, states=GRID_STATES),
    Role(
        "backup_start_event", "Generator start request and result", "GENERATOR", EVENT_UNIT, "EVENT", ONE, True,
        event_kinds=("START_REQUEST", "START_RESULT"),
    ),
    Role(
        "generator_state", "Generator state", "GENERATOR", "state", "STATE", ONE, True,
        states=GENERATOR_STATES,
    ),
    Role("backup_bus_state", "Backup bus state", "AC_BUS", "state", "STATE", ONE, True, states=BUS_STATES),
    Role(
        "grid_power_event", "Grid power loss and restoration", "METER", EVENT_UNIT, "EVENT", ONE, True,
        event_kinds=("POWER_LOSS", "POWER_RESTORED"),
    ),
    Role(
        "transfer_result", "Transfer result", "TRANSFER_SWITCH", EVENT_UNIT, "EVENT", ONE, False,
        event_kinds=("TRANSFER_RESULT",),
    ),
    Role(
        "door_event", "Door open and close", "COLD_ROOM", EVENT_UNIT, "EVENT", PER_ASSET, False,
        event_kinds=("DOOR_OPEN", "DOOR_CLOSE"),
    ),
    Role("ambient_temperature", "Ambient temperature", "TEMPERATURE_PROBE", "degC", "NUMBER", PER_ASSET, False),
    Role(
        "manual_temperature", "Manual temperature log", "COLD_ROOM", "degC", "EVENT", PER_ASSET, False,
        event_kinds=("MANUAL_LOG",),
    ),
)

#: The Foundation property each cold policy role is selected from.
COLD_POLICY_PROPERTIES: dict[str, str] = {
    "cold_policy_low": "cold-policy-low",
    "cold_policy_high": "cold-policy-high",
}
#: W2-01 publishes this fixed `cold-roles-1` channel contract, never a stored
#: or observed cadence (`D-2026-10-17-w2-01-answers` D-I): periodic channels
#: sample every 300 s; EVENT channels are transition-driven (no cadence).
COLD_PERIODIC_CADENCE_SECONDS = 300

ROLES_BY_SET: dict[str, tuple[Role, ...]] = {
    ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_1: MINIGRID_ROLES,
    ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_2: MINIGRID_ROLES_2,
    ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_3: MINIGRID_ROLES_3,
    ROLE_SET_COLD_1: COLD_ROLES_1,
}
#: The role set a new publication for a Site type is recorded under.
ROLE_SET_BY_SITE_TYPE: dict[str, str] = {"MINIGRID": ROLE_SET_MINIGRID, "COLDCHAIN": ROLE_SET_COLD_1}
#: Every role set a Site type still serves: a binding set recorded under an
#: older version stays interpretable (and selectable as a historical basis)
#: under the role definitions it was published with, never today's.
SERVED_ROLE_SETS_BY_SITE_TYPE: dict[str, frozenset[str]] = {
    "MINIGRID": frozenset({ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_1, ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_2, ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_3}),
    "COLDCHAIN": frozenset({ROLE_SET_COLD_1}),
}

#: S6-02a verification inputs (Architect S6-02a Q2). Interval-power roles

#: Names the wording below; any wording change is a new version.
VERIFICATION_READINESS_VERSION = "verification-readiness-1"

def serves(site_type: str, role_set: str) -> bool:
    """Whether a Site of `site_type` can read bindings recorded under `role_set`."""
    return role_set in SERVED_ROLE_SETS_BY_SITE_TYPE.get(site_type, frozenset())

#: S1 publishes numeric IDENTITY and generator-state ENUM only (decision Q4).
#: LINEAR is deferred until supported unit pairs, coefficients and
#: normalization rules are decided; a LINEAR conversion is refused.
CONVERSION_KINDS = ("IDENTITY", "ENUM")
DEFERRED_CONVERSION_KINDS = ("LINEAR",)


def roles_for(role_set: str) -> dict[str, Role]:
    return {role.role: role for role in ROLES_BY_SET.get(role_set, ())}


def conversion_problem(role: Role, source_unit: str, conversion: object) -> str | None:
    """Why this conversion cannot turn `source_unit` into the role's unit, or None."""
    assets = {str(a.id): a for a in registrations.assets}
    sources = {
        s.source_external_id: s
        for s in registrations.sources
        if gateways and s.gateway_id == gateways[0].id
    }
    declared = [(_declared_signals(row.content), row) for row in foundation]
    if not foundation:
        problem(
            "BINDING_FOUNDATION_MISSING", None,
            "No currently recorded Foundation period covers this binding period.",
        )

    # ONE means one per Site and never a choice among several (decision Q1,
    # review M2): a Foundation period with more than one eligible component for
    # a ONE role it needs is refused, not resolved by picking one.
    requested_roles = {req.role for req in requests}
    for row in foundation:
        components = list(row.content.get("components") or ())
        for role in roles.values():
            if role.cardinality != "ONE" or not (role.required or role.role in requested_roles):
                continue
            if role.critical_load:
                continue  # the critical-load-priority control names the one asset
            eligible = sorted(c["component_id"] for c in components if c.get("component_type") == role.asset_type)
            if len(eligible) > 1:
                problem(
                    "BINDING_ROLE_AMBIGUOUS", None,
                    f"{role.label} is one per Site in {role_set}, but Foundation v{row.version} declares "
                    f"{len(eligible)} {role.asset_type} components ({', '.join(eligible)}); this release "
                    "does not choose among them.",
                )

    rows: list[BindingRow] = []
    channels: set[tuple[str, str]] = set()
    single: dict[tuple[str, str | None], int] = {}
    for index, req in enumerate(requests):
        v4_mapping = None
        channel = (req.device_id, req.signal_id)
        name = f"{req.device_id} / {req.signal_id}"
        if channel in channels:
            problem("BINDING_CHANNEL_OVERLAP", index, f"Channel {name} is bound more than once in this set.")
        channels.add(channel)
        source = sources.get(req.device_id)
        if source is None:
            problem(
                "BINDING_SOURCE_UNKNOWN", index,
                f"Device {req.device_id!r} is not a registered source of {site.site_code}'s gateway.",
            )
        for signals, row in declared:
            unit = signals.get(channel)
            if unit is None:
                problem(
                    "BINDING_SIGNAL_UNDECLARED", index,
                    f"Foundation v{row.version} declares no signal {name}.",
                )
            elif unit != req.source_unit:
                problem(
                    "BINDING_UNIT_MISMATCH", index,
                    f"{name} is declared in {unit!r} in Foundation v{row.version}, not {req.source_unit!r}.",
                )
        asset = None
        if req.asset_id is not None:
            asset = assets.get(req.asset_id)
            if asset is None:
                problem(
                    "BINDING_ASSET_UNKNOWN", index,
                    f"Asset {req.asset_id} is not a registered asset of {site.site_code}.",
                )
        role: Role | None = roles.get(req.role)
        if role is None:
            problem("BINDING_ROLE_UNKNOWN", index, f"Role {req.role!r} is not in {role_set}.")
        elif role.covered_by != "CHANNEL":
            problem(
                "BINDING_ROLE_NOT_A_CHANNEL", index,
                f"{role.label} is covered by a Foundation control, not by a channel binding.",
            )
        else:
            if req.canonical_unit != role.canonical_unit:
                problem(
                    "BINDING_UNIT_MISMATCH", index,
                    f"{role.label} is stated in {role.canonical_unit!r}, not {req.canonical_unit!r}.",
                )
            why = conversion_problem(role, req.source_unit, req.conversion)
            if why is not None:
                problem("BINDING_CONVERSION_INVALID", index, f"{name}: {why}.")
            if role_set == ROLE_SET_COLD_1:
                # W2-01 (W2-00 review Low 2, design section 3 channel closure): a channel of the closed v4
                # table serves exactly the role it names, with exactly the role's event kinds.
                entry = V4_CHANNEL_MAPPINGS.get(channel)
                if entry is not None and entry.role != role.role:
                    problem(
                        "BINDING_CHANNEL_ROLE_MISMATCH", index,
                        f"Channel {name} serves {entry.role or 'no cold role'}, not {role.role}.",
                    )
                elif entry is not None and set(entry.allowed_record_kinds) != set(role.event_kinds):
                    problem(
                        "BINDING_CHANNEL_ROLE_MISMATCH", index,
                        f"Channel {name} carries {sorted(entry.allowed_record_kinds)}, but {role.label} "
                        f"needs {sorted(role.event_kinds)}.",
                    )
                elif v4_metadata and entry is None:
                    problem("BINDING_CHANNEL_V4_UNKNOWN", index,
                            f"Channel {name} is not in the closed v4 channel table.")
                elif v4_metadata and entry.family != ("EVENT" if role.value_kind == "EVENT" else "MEASUREMENT"):
                    problem("BINDING_CHANNEL_ROLE_MISMATCH", index,
                            f"Channel {name} carries {entry.family} records, not {role.label} values.")
                elif v4_metadata and entry.family == "MEASUREMENT" and req.canonical_unit not in entry.allowed_units:
                    problem("BINDING_UNIT_MISMATCH", index,
                            f"Channel {name} carries {sorted(entry.allowed_units)}, not {req.canonical_unit!r}.")
                elif v4_metadata:
                    v4_mapping = entry.metadata()
            if req.asset_id is None:
                problem("BINDING_ASSET_REQUIRED", index, f"{role.label} must name the asset it describes.")
            elif asset is not None and asset.asset_type != role.asset_type:
                problem(
                    "BINDING_ROLE_ASSET_MISMATCH", index,
                    f"{role.label} describes a {role.asset_type} asset; {asset.asset_code} is {asset.asset_type}.",
                )
            if role.critical_load and asset is not None:
                for row in foundation:
                    first = critical_load_code(row.content)
                    if first != asset.asset_code:
                        problem(
                            "BINDING_CRITICAL_LOAD_MISMATCH", index,
                            f"{role.label} must name the first load of the critical-load priority in Foundation "
                            f"v{row.version} ({first or 'not configured'}), not {asset.asset_code}.",
                        )
            if role.role == "battery_soc" and asset is not None:
                # The state of charge and the reserve control must describe one battery.
                for row in foundation:
        )
        messages = document["readiness"]["messages"]
    except (KeyError, TypeError, ValueError, AttributeError) as error:
        return [ConfigurationGap(
            "COLD_FOUNDATION_UNEVALUABLE", "foundation",
            f"The selected cold Foundation revision cannot be evaluated ({type(error).__name__}).")]
    return [ConfigurationGap(m["code"], m["role"] or "foundation", m["message"])
            for m in messages if m["blocking"]]


def acquisition_snapshot(
    tenant_id: UUID,
    site: StoredSite,
    revisions: Sequence[RevisionRow],
    registrations: Registrations,
    binding_set: BindingSetRevisionRow | None,
    *,
    now: datetime | None = None,
) -> Snapshot | SnapshotUnavailable:
    """The pinned gateway snapshot of the binding set selected, or why there is none.

    The bytes served are the canonical bytes stored in the publishing
    transaction, checked against the SHA-256 stored with them (decision Q6). They
    are never rebuilt from current registration rows, whose identity fields can
    change after publication (S1-02 fix round, review M1); a stored hash the
    stored bytes do not reproduce is refused, never served.

    Current versus historical (D-2026-10-08-s2-lab-snapshot-selection-and-run-boundary):
    when `now` is given and the selected set is no longer the current
    acquisition basis (`is_current_snapshot`), gaps derived from CURRENT
    mutable rows (`CURRENT_ROW_GAP_CODES`) do not withdraw it. Its original
    registration identity is validated from the stored snapshot instead, and
    the current gaps are reported in `current_gaps`. Gaps of the selected
    Foundation content always refuse, and so does every gap of a current set.
    Without `now` every selection is treated as current, the S1-02 behaviour.
    """
    if binding_set is None:
        return SnapshotUnavailable(
            "ACQUISITION_SNAPSHOT_UNAVAILABLE",
            "No channel-link set is in effect for the period shown, so no gateway snapshot exists.",
            409,
        )
    foundation = next((r for r in revisions if r.id == binding_set.foundation_revision_id), None)
    if foundation is None:
        return SnapshotUnavailable(
            "ACQUISITION_SNAPSHOT_INCONSISTENT",
            f"Binding v{binding_set.version} names a Foundation revision this Site does not hold.",
            500,
        )
    current = now is None or is_current_snapshot(binding_set, now)
    if site.site_type == "COLDCHAIN":
        # q6 (D-2026-10-17-w2-02-buildspec): COLDCHAIN readiness is the W2-01 cold rule on the selected
        # revision and binding set; the MINIGRID gap rule is never applied to it. Every blocking cold
        # readiness message refuses, current or historical.
        gaps = cold_snapshot_gaps(foundation, registrations, binding_set)
        blocking = gaps
    else:
        gaps = configuration_gaps(site, foundation, registrations)
        blocking = gaps if current else [g for g in gaps if g.code not in CURRENT_ROW_GAP_CODES]
    if blocking:
        return SnapshotUnavailable(
            "ACQUISITION_SNAPSHOT_UNAVAILABLE",
            "This Foundation has configuration gaps ("
            + ", ".join(g.code for g in blocking)
            + "), so no gateway snapshot can be downloaded.",
            409,
        )
    if binding_set.snapshot_schema not in SERIALIZERS:
        return SnapshotUnavailable(
            "ACQUISITION_SNAPSHOT_SCHEMA_UNSUPPORTED",
            f"Snapshot schema {binding_set.snapshot_schema!r} has no serializer in this build.",
            500,
        )
    body = binding_set.snapshot_canonical
    if not body:
        return SnapshotUnavailable(
